| 23a | **Task 23's 12 review notes** (non-blocking) - six are real design defects in an **Architect-owned** file | todo | **Classes (Task 38): G = wrong or visible for a player · T = a test that can pass when it should fail · D = docs/wording · L = later/before release.** **All L or D, and most of them are Architect-owned** (the Builder never edits `docs/design/`, rule 3): (a)-(f) L, for the next shotgun regeneration; (g)-(i) D, same file; (j) **done** -- CLAUDE.md documents `reviews/task-<N>/BRIEF.md`; (k) D; (l) L, the Director's ROADMAP row. The Builder never edits `docs/design/` (rule 3), so these go to the **next regeneration of the shotgun design, or to the Builder at build time with the Director's say-so**. Needs an ignore-list parameter or a per-shot cast. **(b)** `table.freeze` is shallow: `CONFIG.SPREAD_FULL_DEG` and the replica's `barrels`/`loaded` stay writable, so "frozen, no writer" and the spec that asserts a write errors both only hold at the top level. **(c)** No `Players.PlayerRemoving` cleanup for per-player `WeaponState` and the rate-limit counters: a leak that pins the Player instance. **(d)** `ActionRequest` must accept only `"Reload"` and `"SelectAmmo"`; as written the server-only primitives `Break`/`Load`/`Close` look reachable from a client. **(e)** The safety arc is evaluated on the client's camera direction, not the muzzle direction the pellets take. **(f)** `weapon_shot.spec` item 2 contradicts its own preamble (imports `CONFIG`) and its "at least one direction beyond `SPREAD_FULL_DEG/4`" is ~25% flaky for `Slug`, which returns one direction - seed it and apply that half to `Buck`. Six screenshots inspected. **Karen played it 2026-09-25: "all good"** (`PLAYTEST.md`) - head and chest kill, a leg hit escapes, the flash plus the bolt read as a hit, the marker reads, two body hits kill, and no sound was asked for. Feel values stay the design defaults, zone tint on. Merged as PR #24 |

What keeps it walkable is arithmetic: `SCATTER_JITTER` 0.9 -> **0.5** guarantees 22 x 0.5 = 11 studs between neighbouring candidates, and the spec MEASURES the real minimum over five seeds at **11.30 studs**, a 6.3-stud clear gap past the widest trunk against the boar's AgentRadius = 2. **N2 (the measurement Director decision F was about): 25/25 PathStatus.Success with 2,746 trunks standing**, `reach` 12.5 s wall clock including the 6 s navmesh settle (~0.26 s per ComputeAsync) -- no density fallback needed and no row proposing one. **N3: 273 steps, 2,402 ms of step time** (terrain 1,975 ms over 256 tiles; the wood 298 ms over 4 quadrant steps; brush 30 ms), **6,264 parts** against the design's ~6,650 estimate: 2,746 trees (ceiling 3,000), 500 brush, 234 hedge. Density measured at **17.71 trees/10k studs in the dense band against 0.88 in the backdrop**. Species measured at spruce 39.6 / birch 28.2 / oak 20.5 / alder 11.7 per cent against Karen's 40/25/20/15 (the built map runs more alder because WET GROUND FORCES ALDER, which is the design's rule working). **TWO DEFECTS THE FIRST BUILD FOUND, both mine:** the species came from a bell-shaped noise field indexed into a share table, so the middle band won (230 birch to 195 spruce to 3 oak) -- it is a flat hash of the clump's block now; and every prop step rebuilt its folder, so the four quadrant steps wiped each other and the world held 964 trees while the log said 1,869. Crowns are CanCollide=false / CanQuery=true (the navmesh sees trunks, a shot still stops in the canopy); brush is neither. The two stale v2 tracks are gone (row 58a(d)); one flank track at x = 880 remains **ROUND 1 = 1 BLOCKING FINDING, and it is PROJECT_CONTEXT's named killer: one predicate answered two unrelated questions.** `Scatter.weighted` stored the key that decided WHETHER a candidate becomes a tree on the point, and `Scatter.speciesAt` used that same number to decide WHICH species it is -- so every candidate accepted where the density is 0.42 (the drive) or 0.14 (the backdrop) had a key below `SPECIES_CLUMP_STRENGTH = 0.65` and always got the clump's leader: **every clump outside the dense band was a pure monoculture**, about half the wood. **Round 2 gives each question its own number, and makes the reuse impossible rather than wrong:** `Scatter.pointKey(x, z, seed, stream)` is the point's own number, hashed from WHERE it is; `speciesAt` no longer TAKES a key, so no caller can hand it one drawn for something else; the acceptance key no longer leaves `Scatter.weighted` (a point is `{x, z}`); and `Scatter.clumpLeaderAt` is exposed because "which clump is this" is a question of its own. The hash is the one `clumpHash` already used -- **measured flat before it was used for a second question** (200,000 points land within 0.05 pp of 10 % in every decile) -- so no clump leader and **no tree** moved: the same seed rebuilds the same 2,746 trees, quadrant for quadrant, and only the species assignment differs (spruce 1082->1090, birch 542->567, oak 530->522, alder 592->567). **THE AGGREGATE MIX DOES NOT CATCH THIS BUG** -- the leader is itself drawn by share, so the marginal shares are unbiased either way, which is why round 1's share assertion passed over a broken wood. The new spec measures the share of trees that are NOT their clump's leader, PER DENSITY TIER, against the derived `(1 - strength) * (1 - sum share^2)` = 0.2503: thin 0.262 over 1,438 trees, dense 0.244 over 1,289, 0.018 apart, and every species' share asserted in each tier (thin 0.401/0.266/0.202/0.131, dense 0.406/0.281/0.205/0.109 against Karen's 0.40/0.25/0.20/0.15). **MUTATION-CHECKED with the bug itself**: scaling the species number by the density again makes the thin tier read 0.000 and fails the tier-agreement line, while the old share assertion passes in the same run. Rebuilt at seed 1: `mapgen verify` **25/25 `PathStatus.Success`**, same seed twice same digest `940540037013158d...`, `contract OK`; `[harness] PASS: 30/30 @ a2ebfca` (clean tree); four screenshots retaken and inspected; the map CLEARED again (`cellsAfter: 0`, `paletteRestored: true`, census shows Camera and Terrain only) |

| 72 | **round 2** | in review | **The Director looked at the renders and found a real defect the Builder's report had described as good** (rule 8). The gun came out in black-and-orange camouflage with mirror-chrome barrels. **The cause was one line**: `image_array` flips the image so row 0 is the top, and `rasterise` indexed the region masks with the UV `v` coordinate directly -- so every mask was the VERTICAL MIRROR of the pixels it edited, and the barrels' near-black was painted onto wood texels and the walnut onto the barrels. **Every colour still reported dead on target**, because the median was measured through the same mirrored mask. **It was NOT the decimation** (the Director's hypothesis, and mine): with no decimation at all the damage measured 2.00x the source's edge density against 1.92x decimated. Four checks now catch the class -- the untouched source is rendered at the same four cameras every run, an edge-density ratio compares them (1.92 then, **0.37-0.88** now), each region's colour is sampled back THROUGH THE MESH by a different route than the mask that wrote it (drift over 45/255 fails), and the region map's speckle is measured. Also fixed: classification sampled ONE texel per triangle (seven now, median, then neighbour-smoothed); roughness and metallic were PAINTED into the shared maps through triangle masks (1.4x on their own with the albedo untouched -- they are material values now); masks are feathered. Barrels RGB(26,28,34) **metallic 0.30 / roughness 0.55** (round 1 had those two the wrong way round, which is a mirror). **NO DECIMATION BY DEFAULT**: Roblox allows 20,000 per mesh and this arrives at 19,325, and at 6,000 the wrist facets and the barrel/forend join grows black wedges. Selftest **45 checks**; the upside-down mask is now a mutation the selftest fails on (drift 177) |

| 76 | **The early joiner gets her own shotgun** | awaiting review | Branch `task-76-early-join-gun`, from `main` (`25da32d`). **THE DEFECT (row 75a(h), measured twice):** `WeaponBoot` starts the weapon system BEFORE the preload -- Task 74 review round 1 asked for exactly that, because a stalled `LoadAsset` must not mean a gunless player -- and the preload took **1227 ms**. Nothing rebuilt a Tool that was granted before it, so a player who spawned in the server's first second kept Task 71's parts gun for as long as they held it. In Studio Play a join takes well under a second, so **in every playtest Karen has ever run she was holding the fallback**, not her own uploaded shotgun. **THE FIX IS AT THE CLASS, in the one owner** (`src/server/Weapon/Hardware.luau`, the only writer of Tool Instances): `Hardware.upgrade(tool)` is a single path that puts the mesh on a gun already in a hand, and `Hardware.upgradeAll()` sweeps every carried Tool. **The worn model IS the state** (`Hardware.MODEL_NAME`), so idempotence is structural rather than a flag: a second arrival, the boot line and the sweep all find the `Model` child and do nothing -- two guns in one hand is not reachable. **The mesh goes on BEFORE the parts come off**, so a template that never arrives changes nothing and nobody is ever left holding an empty Handle; `removeParts` destroys exactly the pieces `Shape.pieces` names, by name, so the `Handle`, the `Muzzle` attachment every shot is measured from, the grip and the `ModelWeld` cannot be caught by it. **No weapon state is touched**: a player mid-reload or mid-aim sees the look change and nothing else. **TWO CALLERS, and the second is the net:** `WeaponBoot` upgrades once the moment the preload returns, and `Weapon`'s existing 2 s arming sweep repeats it -- because a load that timed out is late rather than lost (`Loader` adopts it) and there is no signal for that arrival (counted as `Weapon.stats().meshUpgrades`). **THE ADS VIEWMODEL FOLLOWS FROM ITS ONE SOURCE:** `Camera.Viewmodel` clones the Handle and used to rebuild only when the source part changed IDENTITY, which an in-place upgrade never does -- so the first-person gun would have kept the parts fallback while the third-person gun wore the mesh. It now watches the source's `ChildAdded`/`ChildRemoved` and rebuilds when they fire; it is told nothing about what a gun is made of. **Tested by parameter, never by a clock**: a provider that answers nil and then a template (which is what 'late' is from this module's side) upgrades a tool built before it exactly once; a provider that never answers leaves the whole parts gun; a Tool with no Handle is refused rather than erroring in a sweep; and one case runs with the LIVE provider and asserts **no carried gun in this server wears the fallback** -- the assertion row 75a(h) failed. A client spec drives the viewmodel rebuild. **`withLoader` in `assets_seam.spec` now unwires the template provider too**: the sweep asks for a template every 2 s, and the yielding cases would otherwise let it weld the spec's fake onto a live player's gun. **AND THE FIX FOUND A SECOND DEFECT, which is why this took three commits:** `069e430` PASSED one harness run and FAILED the next. `WeaponBoot` wired the template provider AFTER `preload`, and `preload` waits for a load running in another thread -- so the template is cached a frame or more before `preload` returns, and through that frame `Loader.template` answered with a mesh while `Hardware` had no way to ask for one. The harness plans its specs inside that window about half the time, and `assets_seam.spec` captured its 'live' provider at PLAN time and put that value back after every case: **nil**. A server with no provider builds the parts gun for every grant and cannot upgrade one either, silently, for the rest of the session -- including in any screenshot taken afterwards. The seam is wired **before** the network now (it costs nothing: `Loader.template` never yields and answers nil until the load lands), the spec borrows at the moment of the borrow, and it asserts the seam is still wired after every case. **The sweep's `pcall` no longer swallows its error** (`meshUpgradeFails`, warned once): 'repaired nothing' and 'erroring every pass' looked identical, which is what made this hard to read. **FOUR MUTATIONS applied, run and restored:** the upgrade removed (two cases fail, and the live note then reads `0 wearing the mesh` with the provider wired -- the defect itself); the idempotence guard removed (the 'exactly once' assertion fails); `removeParts` skipped ('Expected nil, got BarrelLeft'); the Viewmodel `stale` check removed (the client spec fails). **THE PLAYER'S PATH, MEASURED:** in the `80380a7` run the note read `1 carried gun(s), 0 wearing the mesh, live template=yes, provider answers=yes` -- the harness's own player was holding the fallback -- and the case passes because the upgrade fixes it. Three Play screenshots of that run show the mesh gun in ADS and in hand. `[harness] PASS: 30/30 @ 80380a7` (clean tree); **408 server specs** (404 before: four new), 86 client (85 before: one new). |

it splits it into two single-channel maps now. **The split hands back its PIXELS, and that is the defect of the task**: a `bpy.data.images.new` datablock is GENERATED, its buffer is Blender's to free, and across the region and mask pass it comes back BLACK -- the split was provably correct tested on its own (roughness 0.6196, metalness 0.0000) while the run it was part of wrote a roughness map of zeros, with every number in the report agreeing with itself. **A BAND BOUNDARY IS SOFTENED ON THE MODEL, NOT IN THE ATLAS**: blurring the finished mask by the 110 texels a boar's saddle really fades over made every region's mask cover the whole 2048 map, because a generator packs unrelated UV islands a few texels apart (the snout finished 59 off target) -- so a face near a band edge gets a partial weight in both bands, and a ramp may only reach another SOFT band, or the flank's would paint coat colour over the tusks at half the animal's height. **`valueSpread`, not a value ratio, for the coat**: Meshy bakes AO into the albedo and a ratio correction multiplies the already-dark pixels down with the median -- the first run came back near-black from every camera (rule 5, and it took two more runs to get right). **Targets** (Director's pick, Karen's to change): back **104, 95, 84**, flank **158, 147, 130** -- 1.5x the back, which is the step a hunter reads at eighty metres -- belly and legs 86, snout 58, tusks 208 dull bone; roughness 0.88/0.85/0.88/0.55/0.45, metalness 0 everywhere, maps out at **2048**. Every region landed: drifts 6-13 of 255 against a ceiling of 45, shine within 0.04 of 0.08. | 78 | **The harness bug: a flag added this week did not exist in Edit mode** | awaiting review | Branch `task-78-flags-require-cache`, from `main` (`3273f3e`). `[harness] PASS: 32/32 @ 4f28c0b` (clean tree) -- **32, not 30: two new checks**. **THE BUG, in the Director's own words (Task 77):** `python tools/flags.py set ORANGE_OUTFITS on` refused *"no such flag"* in Edit mode while a Play session resolved that same flag fine, and `flags` did not list it at all -- so the Director could not switch a flag on for Karen's playtest, which is the one thing `docs/design/feature-flags.md` exists to make cheap. **THE CAUSE, quoted from Roblox's own reference:** *"ModuleScripts run once and only once per Luau environment and return the exact same value for subsequent calls to require()"*, and return values are *"independent with regards to Scripts and LocalScripts, and other environments like the Command Bar"* -- the Edit-mode MCP context is one of those other environments and keeps a cache of its own, and **Rojo rewrites the SOURCE of the same ModuleScript rather than making a new one**, so the first require of a Studio session is what every later call gets back. **BOTH RENDERED DOC PAGES GAVE A FETCHER NOTHING** (asked twice); the sentences are quoted from the generated YAML those pages are built from, and the rule this repo set for exactly that (the Blender manual, `en.help.roblox.com`) was followed: say the page did not answer, and **MEASURE**. Built from scratch, in memory, in the same context `flags set` runs in: require a ModuleScript declaring **A**, rewrite its Source to declare **A and B** as Rojo does, require the SAME instance -> still **A**; require a **Clone** -> **A,B**. **THE FIX WAS ALREADY IN THIS REPO (rule 2):** `tools/mapgen.py`'s `CALL` has used `require(source:Clone())` since 2026-09-26 with the same measurement written beside it -- the flag queries had simply never got it, and the comment on `QUERY_FLAG_TABLE` asserted the OPPOSITE, that the cache was *"CORRECT rather than a hazard"*. A wrong comment beside working code is how a defect survives a review. **What ships:** one `FRESH_FLAGS` snippet shared by both Edit-mode queries that need `Flags.DEFAULTS` -- a parentless clone, required, destroyed -- which keeps them free of arbitrary Luau (still a constant with a JSON name and a boolean literal) and puts no script in any Rojo-owned container, so the harness's own *no script outside Rojo-managed paths* check cannot trip over it. **Rejected and said why**: parsing `Source` (a second, weaker implementation of what the module already says), `loadstring` (arbitrary Luau), parenting the clone (measured to work, and it is the one thing that check exists to catch). **TWO HARNESS CHECKS, about different things**: the in-memory fixture proving a clone reads the current source -- which asserts only that half, and *reports* whether the same instance went stale, so a future Roblox fix cannot block this repo -- and Studio's flag table compared against the names parsed out of `src/shared/Flags/init.luau` by a different route, which is the check that catches a reverted call site. **THREE OFFLINE SELFTEST CHECKS** for the parser (it finds a newly added flag, and does not mistake `NAME_PATTERN`/`MAX_FLAGS`/`DEFAULTS` for flags), so CI covers the half that needs no Studio. **TWO MUTATIONS applied, run and restored**: the cached require put back -> the flag-table check fails naming the missing `ORANGE_OUTFITS`, the bug itself; the fixture stopped cloning -> `fresh` reads `A` and the mechanism check fails. **AND IT WAS DONE FOR REAL, in the same stale Studio session that had refused it minutes earlier**: `set ORANGE_OUTFITS on` -> `override set: ORANGE_OUTFITS=true`, `flags` lists all three with `override on` / `effective on`, `clear` -> `cleared: DHFlag_ORANGE_OUTFITS`. Research note `docs/research/2026-09-27-require-cache.md` (rule 1) with the full audit of every `require` in `tools/`. **ROUND 2, AND THE FINDING WAS RIGHT: THE FIX HAD THE SAME SHAPE AS THE BUG.** Round 1's `FRESH_FLAGS` answered a bare `"REFUSED: ..."` string; the `set` path checked that prefix and the two `json.loads` call sites did not, so the REACHABLE case -- Studio open in Edit before Rojo has connected, which is exactly what the message names -- ended in a `JSONDecodeError` traceback where it used to print `[flags] could not read ...`, and the handler written for it became dead code (the diff had removed the only producer of `{error=...}`). Right about the thing it aimed at, wrong about the thing beside it. **Fixed in TWO places, not one:** every query built on `FRESH_FLAGS` answers a JSON object with failure as `{error=...}`, and `QUERY_SET_FLAG` answers `{set="NAME=true"}` on success so no second shape survives; and `json_answer()` is the ONE parser, which turns a bare string, truncated JSON, `null`, a Luau error text or an array into a problem the caller prints -- so a caller cannot make that mistake again whatever a future query answers. `QUERY_REQUIRE_CACHE` goes through it too. **Checked OFFLINE, in CI:** the real `run_flags` is driven against a scripted Studio for **five bad answers and two good ones**, asserting the message and the exit code (1 for `show`, 2 for `set`) rather than the absence of a crash, plus a source-shape check that no Flags query returns a bare string. **Two more mutations, applied, run and restored**: round 1's call site restored -> four cases report `raised JSONDecodeError`, the defect verbatim; round 1's bare-string refusal restored -> the shape check fails. `[harness] PASS: 32/32 @ 314337b` (clean tree), and `set`/`clear` re-proved live against the new shape. **Three non-blocking notes fixed in the same round**: the docstring's ordered check list gains the two new checks and is renumbered (CLAUDE.md makes that docstring the source of truth for every check); the parser selftest is driven by a SYNTHETIC `DEFAULTS` table instead of hardcoding `ORANGE_OUTFITS`, which would have failed CI the day that flag is legitimately retired and did not test what its label claimed; and the research note's sources carry licence and maintenance status, with the shortfall against rule 1's "3+ external" said plainly rather than padded. |

| 80 | **weapon_client's intermittent failure: a test fault, measured** | **NEEDS TEST2** | Branch `task-80-weapon-client-flake`, stacked on `task-79-map-switch` (`5d31119`); the PR targets that branch, because Task 79 is not merged. **THE QUESTION WAS WHETHER IT IS A GAME BUG, AND IT IS NOT.** The dispatch named the alarming reading: a refused or exploit fire request that still spends a shell would be a security defect, not flakiness. It is a TEST fault, and the measurement is unambiguous. Six runs of `test` on the map world at `e9911c2` went PASS, FAIL, PASS, PASS, PASS, FAIL, and the failing run's own note names the cause: `shots seen: 3 ... cue at +4.5s; 3 gun(s) handed over [+2.2s backpack, +41.7s backpack, +41.8s backpack]; rises [#1 at +4.1s None/Slug res=24/24 BEFORE THE CUE, #2 at +7.3s None/Slug res=23/24, #3 at +8.3s None/None res=23/24]`. **A slug fired four tenths of a second BEFORE the spec's own cue**, from a full gun. Nothing forged was ever accepted: the exploit window came back identical before and after in every one of those runs -- ten snapshots each, all `busyFor=0.00`. **ONE CAUSE, AND IT IS THE GAME BEHAVING CORRECTLY.** `Weapon.grant` ends with `Hardware.equip` because `Shotgun.CONFIG.AUTO_EQUIP` is true (Karen: a playtest should not start by hunting for the hotbar), and `Weapon.refreshArming`'s sweep calls grant again whenever it finds a player who should be holding one -- three guns per one-player session, nine in the failing two-player one. `weapon_client`'s `parkTool` returned true the moment it found the gun in the Backpack ("already parked") **without unequipping anything**, and the owner auto-equipped it a moment later. Then the replay's first click fired it. The spec treated a one-off act as an invariant against an owner that is free to undo it. **FIXED WHERE THE ASSUMPTION IS.** (1) Parking is an invariant: a watcher unequips the gun every time the owner hands one over, until the cue. (2) This spec's window has a NEAR end -- `ownLog()` starts at the cue, so a shot before it is not counted as its own. (3) The reserve assertions are a DELTA from what the gun held at the cue, not `== 24`, which was `Shotgun.CONFIG`'s starting pocket and therefore a claim about the whole session. (4) The exploit window is thrown away if the owner handed over a gun inside it -- causal, and it does not care how long anything took; a grant resets the state and auto-equips, which is exactly what an accepted `Break` looks like, and the two failing two-player runs had `weapon granted=9 revoked=4`. **AND THE PROPERTY MOVED TO THE SERVER, WHERE IT CAN BE DETERMINISTIC**, as the dispatch asked. `Weapon.onActionRequest` and `Weapon.onFireRequest` are the same functions `Weapon.start` connects to the remotes, now reachable by parameter; `weapon_shot.spec` drives twelve forged payloads through them and requires `stats.badRequests` to rise by exactly twelve with `rateDropped` and `shots` unmoved (`weapon_shot: 12 forged request(s) at the handler -> badRequests +12, rateDropped +0, shots +0`). A legitimate grant or reload cannot raise that counter, so there is no window and no waiting. The client case stays as the half only a client can prove: that the remotes REACH that handler. **77a(a) IS RETIRED, AND ITS DIAGNOSIS WAS WRONG.** `zz_outfit_client.spec` is `outfit_client.spec` again. Inserting a file changed WHICH shot leaked into `weapon_client`'s count; it did not cause the leak. Task 80 reproduced the identical failure -- 23 slugs, three shots -- with no new file at all. **EVIDENCE: nine green runs of `test` on the map world at `40390b3`**, one of them (run b) with the exact failing shape, the gun in the Backpack at +2.2 s. **One mutation, applied, run and restored**: `onActionRequest` stops consulting the whitelist -> the server case fails (`badRequests +3` of 12, `weapon_shot.spec:158`) AND the client case fails at `expect(after.open).to.equal(before.open)` -- so the forged `Break` really does open the gun when the guard is gone, and neither half is dead. Every print in `weapon_client` that explains a failure is a `TestKit.note` now: the map world's console comes back `[TRUNCATED DUE TO LENGTH LIMIT]` and took the first diagnostic run's evidence with it. **AND THEN MY OWN NEW SERVER CASE MADE THE SAME MISTAKE, which is worth writing down.** The Director's `test2` at `99a2f5a` came back `[harness2] FAIL: 30/32` with `weapon_shot.spec:161 Expected 0, got 1` -- and the case's own note answered it: `12 forged request(s) at the handler -> badRequests +12, rateDropped +0, shots +1`. All twelve forged payloads were refused; the `shots +1` was the SHOOTER's own replayed click, because `stats` is ONE module-level table for the whole server and `after.shots - before.shots` counted every player's shots inside my window. One player never overlapped; two players did. **A global quantity measured across a wall-clock window -- the same disease, in the fix for it.** **FIXED AT THE CLASS: the seam reports its own decision.** `onActionRequest` and `onFireRequest` return nil when the request was acted on and the reason otherwise (`rate-limited`, `untracked`, the validator's own reason, `not-ready`, `empty-pocket`, `no-muzzle`, the reducer's), one at every exit. `OnServerEvent:Connect` discards the return, so the game is unchanged -- but a return value belongs to ONE call and ONE player and nobody can race it. The spec asserts (i) no forged payload came back nil, so none was acted on, and (ii) none came back `rate-limited` or `untracked`, the two exits BEFORE the validator, which is what would make the case green with the whitelist deleted. The rate limiter is no longer dodged by counting to ten and guessing: a call that says `rate-limited` is simply made again in the next window. **THE NOTE NOW SAYS WHAT IT COUNTED AND FROM WHOM**, which the first version could not: `12 forged request(s) at the handler, subject Alhamdulilah824 of roster [Alhamdulilah824(tracked)]; reasons [action Break=bad-request, ... fire #1=bad-type, ...]; server-wide counters over the same window: badRequests +12, rateDropped +0, shots +0 (other players' shots land in these)`. The counters are context and are labelled as server-wide; the per-call reasons are the assertion. **Three green `test` runs at `fd36286`**, and one mutation between the first and the second, applied, run and restored: drop the `Validator.checkRequest` consult -> the note reads `action Break=nil` (the forged Break ACCEPTED) with every later payload `not-ready` because that Break left the gun broken open, `weapon_shot.spec:202` fails, and the client half fails again at `expect(after.open).to.equal(before.open)`. Both halves bite, and the note names the exploit rather than a number. |

The owner is `Match.Body.dress` (`GAME_DESIGN.md`'s placement row) and `match_outfit.spec` passes every run, so what failed is between "the server built a vest" and "the driver's screen shows one" -- the `UpperTorso`/`Torso` fallback, the attachment, `Massless`/`CanCollide`, or whether `Body.dress` runs for a DRIVER at all on the path a real session takes. **It needs its own task and the Director's queue**, not a line under Task 80: it is a gameplay defect in another owner's file, and Task 80 is a test-harness task that must not grow a second subject. That lands **1.98 s** after the last input the spec could see, against `RELOAD_TOTAL = 2.0`: an input just outside a flat 1.5 s quiet period is still publishing state changes half a second into the window, and `stillBusy()` cannot see it because it reads the newest snapshot's `busyFor` and at window-open time the gun WAS idle. **Fixed by taking the number from the gun instead of guessing it:** `QUIET = Shotgun.CONFIG.RELOAD_TOTAL + 1`, one source, and it moves if Karen changes the feel. 80a(a) STAYS OPEN -- this is still a time-based guard; what it guards is a smoke test whose worst failure is a retry, because the property itself is proved on the server. | 123 | **A group is heard as one or two animals, not twenty** | **BUILT, PROVEN IN THE FOREST TEST, GATE BLOCKED** | Karen, 2026-10-05, after playing the Forest Test: *"to many noise, when boars are walking and there is many only 1-2 sounds"*. **MEASURED BEFORE ANY CODE**, counting playing `Sound` instances under `Workspace.Boars` in her own place with her own waves: **31 at once with 36 boars out** -- 13 footstep loops, 14 grunts, 8 breaths, 4 sniffs. **AND WHY TASK 120'S PER-SOUNDER CAPS HAD NOT FIXED IT: a release of twenty is not a sounder for long.** 23 boars out were THREE sounders holding 3, 3 and 4; the other THIRTEEN were lone boars with full voices, so a rule keyed on MEMBERSHIP governed ten animals of twenty-three. **TWO RATIONS, IN SERIES, all data in `Boar.CONFIG.SOUND.CROWD`.** (1) PER SOUNDER: `VOICES_PER_SOUNDER = 2` members carry the footstep loop and the breathing, `GRUNTS_PER_SOUNDER = 1` -- the GROUP grunts once per `GRUNTS` interval, not each animal, and the BAND is the gap rather than a number of its own (the old `SOUNDER_GRUNT_GAP` was a flat 2.5 s whatever the band said, so a herd muttered every 2.5 s for ever). Holders are the FRONT of the herd -- the same "nearest the exit line" rule its leader is chosen by, so there is ONE answer to where the front is -- and they are STICKY, re-picked only when a holder dies or leaves, because a loop handed between jostling animals stops and restarts. (2) WORLD-WIDE, NEAREST A LISTENER FIRST: `MAX_STEP_VOICES = 3`, `MAX_BREATH_VOICES = 2`, `MAX_MUTTER_VOICES = 2` (grunts and the sniff ride one ceiling: they are the same ambient muttering to a listener). **IN SERIES AND NOT AS AN INTERSECTION, and that was a real defect found by reasoning about the two keys:** the herd ranks its voices at its FRONT while the world ranks by distance to the hunter, and a FLEEING herd's front is its farthest animal from him -- ANDed, the two sets can be disjoint and a whole running sounder goes silent with slots to spare. The sounder gate now comes first and the ceiling is awarded among the survivors. **AND A SLOT ONLY GOES TO AN ANIMAL THAT WOULD USE IT**, which was also measured: the first build gave three samples in a row of TOTAL SILENCE with 40 boars out, because three animals standing at the hunter's feet held every footstep slot while a herd walked past a hundred studs away. Candidacy uses `Body.stepSound`'s own bands, so eligibility and the cue cannot disagree. **THE ONE-SHOTS ARE NOT RATIONED** -- the hit, the cry, the death and the last breath belong to the animal that was shot, and a budget that could silence the animal Karen just hit would hide the one thing a shot has to tell her. Three pure rules carry it so both states are reachable by parameter (`Boar.hasVoice`, `Boar.voiceHolders`, `Boar.nearestVoices`); `Boar.isStepEmitter` and `ONE_STEP_EMITTER` are replaced by them rather than left beside them. **PROVEN, same place and same waves: 31 Sounds playing at once BEFORE, 7 AFTER** (steps 3, breath 2, grunt 2, 29-40 boars out), and **no sample with nothing playing at all**. The 28 pure assertions were also driven against the module the running place actually loaded -- 28/28 -- because the gate could not run. **NOT VERIFIED: the gate, and therefore the four LIVE spec cases in `boar_shot.spec.luau`.** `python tools/studio_mcp.py test` reports `FAIL: 4/5 checks @ 88233dd (clean tree) scope=all`, and the one failure is *"Studio has the DEV place open (130094961655666 vs 136410205938347)"* -- **DEV IS NOT OPEN**, only the Forest Test is, so no spec file ran at all. That is the Director's connect click; the rewritten live cases (the sounder breath A/B, the twelve lone boars, the calm herd's grunts) have never executed. **DEV's own gate was already failing 18/23 on DEV's map before this change** (task 122) and this task does not touch the map. **NO REVIEW THIS ROUND** by the Director's dispatch. **ROUND 2 (2026-10-05), after Karen played `8b71d9b`:** *"Still to much noise / when they cross road or they close they don't make any pig sound besides walking / and I hear only when it;s close inaf to me if it's far away I don't hear / and it shouldnt be chaotic sounds / also they feel to fast once thye spooked it's ok but they seems walking to fast / they has to trot or walk when spook then fast"*. **TWO OF HER FIVE POINTS WERE BUGS WITH A LINE OF CODE BEHIND THEM, NOT DIALS.** (1) **A CROSSING HERD COULD NOT GRUNT AT ALL**: `SOUND.GRUNTS.MAX_SPEED` was 6 studs/s -- `WANDER_SPEED` is 4, so the grunt band was *grazing* -- while a pushed herd moves at 4-18. Every crossing anybody has ever watched was hooves and no pig. It is `MODEL.RUN_FROM` now, defined ONCE as a local and shared with `BREATH.MIN_SPEED` (was 11, a trot) so the two bands MEET instead of leaving the trot band voiceless: walk and trot grunt, run snorts, and `Body.stepSound`'s `if/elseif` gives the meeting point to the grunt so no animal can do both. (2) **THE RANGES WERE INVERTED**: task 120 made the FOOTSTEPS the long-range approach cue (80-130 studs) and left the voice at 85, which is why *"they don't make any pig sound besides walking"* and *"if it's far away I don't hear"* are the same sentence. Steps are 50 studs at 0.30/0.40/0.60; GRUNTS reach 160 at 0.8 on a **60-stud roll-off plateau of their own** -- a new optional `nearStuds` per row, because `InverseTapered` falls as plateau/distance so ONE shared plateau cannot make a voice carry 120 studs and a hoof die at 40. (3) **NOT CHAOTIC**: `VOICES_PER_SOUNDER` 2 -> 1, `MAX_STEP_VOICES` 3 -> 2, and a new `GRUNT_GAP_SECONDS = 1.2` -- a world-wide minimum between grunt STARTS, which is the rule the per-sounder slot cannot express because most of a released group is lone animals. (4) **THE PACE WAS ONE LINE**: `Brain:_step`'s flee branch reads `if threatened then SPRINT_SPEED else TROT_SPEED`, and `threatened` is "anything inside a flush radius" -- which this world's phantom beater is BY CONSTRUCTION (`WAVE.pushStuds` 11 against `SENSE.FLUSH_STUDS` 15). So a pushed herd had no slow pace available at all. `Boar.CONFIG.PACE` (walk 4.5, trot 11 inside 12 studs, spook 30 studs) is a CLAMP after the follower's leader-matching and before the wound's scale: a DRIVER pushes, and a run needs a SPOOK -- `Brain._boltFor` (a heard shot or a hit, both already existing) or a non-driver inside `SPOOK_STUDS`, which `Brain.perceive` now reports as its own `spook` field because `reaction` keeps only the single best cause and the beater is always the closer one. **BORN OFF**, like MODEL/CALM/SENSE, because it changes what a fleeing animal does and the DEV drive is a world of fleeing boars Karen has signed off; `ForestTestBoot` turns it on through `world.config`. (5) **AND A STAMPEDE THAT WAS NOT THE BRAIN AT ALL** -- found by printing the setpoint beside the body speed: `spawnSounder` clamped its ring to `SPAWN_RING_STUDS` = 10, so 20 animals stood 3.1 studs apart with a 5.5-stud body, spawned inside each other, and the solver threw them out at 33-38 studs/s while their own setpoint read 4.5. The ring is now sized to the group (`SOUNDER.SPAWN_SPACING` = 1.2 body lengths of arc) and the Forest Test's `maxSpread` is 90 rather than 30 to leave room for it. **MEASURED, one 120 s Play in the Forest Test, console errors NONE:** crossings at **4.0-4.5 studs/s (walk)** and **11-18 (trot)**; in **260 s of tracing with no shot fired the Brain never asked for more than 18**; two shots through the weapon's own `FireRequest` put **eight animals across the road at 38 (run)** inside six seconds. Sound: **worst 4 Sounds playing at once** (2 steps, 2 grunts) against 7 after round 1 and **31 before the task**; **23 grunt starts, smallest gap 1.30 s** against the 1.2 s floor; **18 of 23 started more than 100 studs from the hunter, out to 337**. **NOT EXPLAINED:** one of four runs showed a burst of eight bodies at ~38 studs/s about twenty seconds before any shot. The box's `CanQuery`, `CanCollide`, `Damageable` and `HitZone`, its three protruding zone parts, the wound model and the sounders are untouched; a real ray fired through the boar in the spec still lands on a zone part and never on the animal. **(4) THE FEET DO NOT SLIDE, AND THAT WAS MEASURED.** Every clip is in place (root-bone drift 0.0000), so a planted hoof's speed IS the clip's ground speed, and all four hooves agreed to three decimals: walk **2.852**, trot **10.244**, run **17.210** studs/s. `Body.clipFor` (pure) plays `speed / that`: 1.40 at `WANDER_SPEED` 4, 1.76 at `TROT_SPEED` 18, 2.21 at `SPRINT_SPEED` 38. Gait thresholds are the midpoints of KAREN's speeds (0.5 / 11 / 28), not the clips' own -- splitting at the clips puts 18 on the run clip and leaves the trot reachable for the 0.13 s `ACCEL` takes to cross its band. Two setters: module-level for the boot (the shape `Weapon.Hardware.setTemplateProvider` already has) and per-runtime for specs, with a `_modelSourceSet` flag so "this runtime has NO template" is expressible -- otherwise the no-model case would be handed the live server's real boar and pass for the wrong reason (Task 76's defect). **(6) THE TEN ANIMATION IDS ARE THE ONE THING A TOOL CANNOT DO**, measured twice: Open Cloud takes an `Animation` only as a Studio-written `.rbxm`/`.rbxmx`, and `AssetService:CreateAssetAsync` answered *"not available yet"* for both `Animation` and `Model` through the harness's plugin context. `ESCALATE.md` has the exact clicks (**NEEDS DIRECTOR CLICKS**) and the tool wrote one FBX per clip to make them mechanical. Every track loaded with the right `Length`, `WeightTarget` went to 1, and `WeightCurrent`, `TimePosition` and every `Bone.Transform` stayed at ZERO for ever on both server and client, with no error. A/B'd live against three copies of one clone: the one keeping the asset's controller never moved; a fresh controller and a Humanoid both animated identically -- so it is two things claiming one rig, not AnimationController vs Humanoid. `attachVisual` strips every controller and animator the template brings and ASSERTS one of each, and the spec's fake template now carries the asset's own controller and the extra Model nesting, because a fake without them let the first spec pass while every boar stood still. **(10) A WOUNDED BOAR WALKED WITH CLAMPED FEET.** The walk/trot boundary was the midpoint of Karen's dials (11), which asks the walk clip for up to 3.86 and got clamped to 2.50 -- a 40 % slide. A healthy boar crosses that band in 0.1 s; a wounded one sits in it, **measured at 8.99 and 9.84 studs/s held for seconds**, because `Wound.speedScale` is a fraction of 18 and not one of the three dials. The band now ends where the walk clip reaches `WALK_MAX_RATE` 1.8 (5.13 studs/s) and the trot takes over near rate 1.0; `MAX_RATE` is 2.8, above the worst real ask (2.73), so it never shapes how an animal moves, and the spec SWEEPS every speed from `WALK_FROM` to `SPRINT_SPEED` and fails if one clamps. **(11) HARNESS FAULT, REPORTED (rule 6):** the manifest passed StudioMCP's ~100 KB result limit and the whole run died as "one instance is too big for a single StudioMCP result" with nothing wrong in the repo. Three causes, all fixed at the cause: the SIGNAL was wrong (yaw smoothed over 0.2 s, the constant derived from dt), the DECISION was bistable (turn enters at 60 deg/s, leaves below 35), and the decision OUTRAN its own crossfade (`MIN_CLIP_SECONDS` 0.18 > `FADE_SECONDS` 0.15, so two tracks is the most that can overlap; a death and a flinch bypass it, and the RATE still follows the speed every frame so a held clip is never at the wrong tempo). A carcass is an unanchored physics body with `CanCollide` on, so walking into it shoves it -- and it is NOT the model's fault: MEASURED with the flag **OFF**, one impulse the size of a walking character (the assembly's own mass 23.1 x 16 studs/s) moved the grey carcass **1.826 studs**. The grey box has been shoveable since Task 28 and nobody had walked into one, so the fix is in BOTH flag states. The box is **anchored once it has come to rest** -- the one thing a walking character cannot move -- and anchoring touches no property a shot reads (`CanQuery`, `CanCollide`, the zone parts and their `HitZone` are unchanged; `Damageable` is false because it is dead, which is Task 28's behaviour). It anchors on STILLNESS, not a timer, because the two states stop at different times: flag OFF has to topple first, flag ON only drops `SPAWN_CLEARANCE`. Thresholds are measured and 30x apart (at rest 0.000-0.003 on both; still toppling peaked at **28.6** linear and **5.6** angular), held for `CARCASS_STILL_SECONDS` 0.25, with a 4 s backstop that REFUSES to fire while the body is still falling -- a boar frozen in mid-air is a worse bug than one that can be nudged. After: the same shove moves it **0.0000 studs** in both states, anchoring took 0.63 s (OFF, after the topple) and 0.52 s (ON), and the box's bottom finished at 619.993 against a ground of 620. Both writes land in one Heartbeat, so every sample of `Speed` reads 0 and the pose looks held. **MEASURED by running round 1's code again with the fix removed: the carcass's Animator had NO playing track at all** -- 0 samples above weight 0.01 over 4 s, server and client -- the non-looped death had been driven past its end and STOPPED, which is the rest pose. With the fix: `deathLeft` at full weight and **Speed 0.000** across 241 samples. Fixed at the cause: `play` asks the new pure `Body.shouldAdjustRate`, which never re-rates the clip the boar is HOLDING; `handle.heldClip` is set once by the freeze and cleared when a new clip starts. The spec counts through that seam -- *600 frames of a frozen death -> 0 rate write(s); unheld -> 600* -- because a live carcass cannot show it (a spec has no loaded animation, so `shouldFreeze` refuses `Length` 0). **Six of the seven notes fixed in the same lines:** the swept band now prints the band it counts (1.2..38); the flag case is renamed to what it asserts and now checks `MODEL.ENABLED` equals `Flags.isOn`; `attachVisual` applies the five passenger properties to EVERY `BasePart` the template brings, not just the mesh; the freeze window is `max(HOLD_EPSILON, speed * dt)` so one long frame cannot step a death past its end; the slice guard compares BYTES against Luau's byte-based `string.sub`; and `TROT_FROM` uses the hoisted `WALK_MAX_RATE` instead of repeating 1.8. The seventh (a multi-byte character split across a slice boundary) is 115a(a) **(16) ROUND 2's FINDING, AND A FALSE MEASUREMENT OF MY OWN, CORRECTED.** The Reviewer: round 2's claim 2 said round 1's code left the rig in the REST POSE, while the same request reused 13:16/13:39 screenshots and Karen's "looks good now" as evidence -- both of pre-fix code. They cannot both be true. **Claim 2 was wrong, and the fault was mine:** the build I measured it on was not round 1's -- I removed the new `heldClip` guard from `play` but left `heldClip` gating the FREEZE, which is a third behaviour that never shipped (re-rate every frame with nothing re-freezing it, so the track really did run away and stop). **Measured on a faithful replica of round 1's three hunks:** `deathLeft` w=1.00 t=1.18/1.21 spd=0.00 at 1 s, 10 s AND 60 s, head +0.42, lowest -0.006 -- identical to this commit, and the frames look the same. So round 1 drew the carcass correctly and Karen's sign-off was never evidence for something that had changed underneath it. What the fix is worth is the COST the Reviewer named: two property writes per frame per carcass, replicated, up to 1,920/s with eight down. Whether a client ever rendered the `speed = 1` it was sent stays the Reviewer's inference -- not observed in either build, and not claimed. **THREE NEW FRAMES, LOOKED AT (rule 5):** `.screenshots/r3b-carcass-{1s,10s,60s}.png` on this code -- the Director was right that a change to how a carcass is HELD is a drawn change and needed a look. **Round 2's six notes:** the redundant 600-iteration case is dropped (it stepped nothing and its note read as a stepped carcass); the freeze window is now the exported `Body.freezeReach`, so the spec tests production and not `math.max`; EVERY drawn part is welded, not just the mesh, now that all of them are forced unanchored; the slice guard reports BYTES and the final reassembly check compares bytes too; and the overshoot on a hitch is written down in `freezeReach`'s own comment as the trade it is. The untested call site (`play`'s arguments and the `heldClip` write) stays untested in a spec and is said so plainly: it needs a loaded animation, which no spec here has |

A `.md` scan was tried for `references` and dropped: a symbol called `references` or `Body` matches English prose, so only code and config are scanned. **(2) THE TESTS.** `python tools/studio_mcp.py test --scope auto` resolves the branch's changed paths (diff against `origin/main` plus anything uncommitted) through `BLAST_RADIUS` to scope names and `SCOPE_SPECS` to spec files; **anything code-like the map does not name resolves to `all`**, never to nothing. What the scope decides is **whether Play happens**: a change that reaches no spec gets the Edit-place checks only (seconds, not ~90 s). It does NOT filter the suite -- `tests/TestKit.luau` loads every spec and this task may not touch `tests/` (113a). The final line carries `scope=all` / `scope=auto:<names>` / `scope=auto:none` (`scope_tag`, and a `--scope auto` run that resolves to the whole suite is tagged `scope=all`, because it IS full evidence), and `tools/agents.py` accepts `auto:` for a review round (and then asks for no `[harness2]` line), accepts `scope=all` and every pre-113 line as full evidence, and REFUSES a hand-named scope -- a chosen scope is a choice, not a measurement. **(3) THE CONTENT LANE.** `CONTENT_PATHS` = `src/shared/Viewmodel/poses.json`: a request made only of it passes the harness gate like a docs change. The ROUND is dropped, not the run -- `tests/server/viewmodel_poses.spec.luau` reads that file, so the content lane's one-player `test` still plays the viewmodel specs. **Proved in CI**, both selftests, 74 new cases between them (`agents.py` 20 -> 77, the harness +17), every one driven by file lists or diff text: a gameplay path can never resolve to "nothing to run", every spec on disk belongs to a scope and every scope names a file that exists, a hand-named scope is not evidence, and a pre-113 line still parses. **(4) `test2` IS NOW FOR THE MATCH, THE DRIVE, THE TIE AND THE TEAMS, AND NOTHING ELSE** (Director decision 2026-10-03, added to this task before the gate, after Karen the same day: *"we dont need 2 player test now we just working on weapon and animal next"*). `TWO_PLAYER_PATHS` was `("src/", "tests/client/", "tools/studio_mcp.py")` -- a human click and eight minutes on every gameplay task -- and is now the explicit list: `src/server/Match/`, `src/server/MatchBoot.server.luau`, `src/client/Match/`, `src/client/MatchBoot.client.luau`, `src/shared/Drive/`, `tests/client/Role.luau`, and the match, tie and outfit specs. Everything else -- weapon, viewmodel, camera, boar, hud, sound, tools -- needs the one-player line only, **for the PR to main as well as for a review round**. `WEAPON_VIEWMODEL_PATHS` is kept and is SUBSUMED, and the selftest proves it inert rather than assuming it. 25 cases drive both directions: 11 paths that must bring the line back, 14 that must not. **Two judgement calls, both written into the code and into CLAUDE.md:** `src/server/Weapon/SafetyArc.luau` is OUT (weapon-side geometry, provable with one player; the penalty it triggers, `src/server/Match/Penalty.luau`, is IN), and `tools/studio_mcp.py` is OUT, which the Director's decision puts with the tools. **The baseline the cost is measured against: 66 review rounds on record, mean $3.36 / 38 turns, median $3.20 / 36.** The after number is this task's own review, which the Director runs |

| 108 | **The hands on model B: measured, and an instrument to see them** | awaiting review | Branch `task-108-hands`, from `content-model-b-aim-1` (`1ee7ba0`), behind `NEW_GUN`. **DISPATCHED BY THE DIRECTOR OUTSIDE THE REPO (2026-10-02, transcribed by the Builder): "TASK 108 (lean lane, viewmodel only, behind NEW_GUN): THE HANDS ON MODEL B HOLD THE GUN LIKE A HUNTER. Karen, after playing: *'gun feels good / hands are bad / left hand is oposit180deg need to turn or not sure but broken'* and *'one arm holds pipes and another stock right ?'*. The Director's own live try of `left.rot.twist 180` moved the glove to the far side of the barrels instead of rolling the palm. DO: 1. `pose.py inspect <pose>` -- capture the viewmodel from a fixed outside camera without moving the player's camera. 2. Measure each v2 glove mesh's own axes and write them as constants, so yaw/pitch/twist mean 'fingers along X, palm toward Y' on BOTH gloves. 3. Seed carry/aim/reload so the right fist is on the stock wrist with the index along the guard and the left palm is up under the forend; no glove intersecting the wood, none above the rib, no open sleeve end facing the camera. 4. Specs on the DRAWN instances. Evidence: `inspect` + `compare`. Gate: one-player if every file is `WEAPON_VIEWMODEL_PATHS`. Karen is away ~1 h and wants this fixed when she is back."** **THE TWO GLOVE MESHES DO NOT SHARE AN AXIS, AND THAT IS KAREN'S BUG.** Measured off each prepped mesh by classifying every vertex by the colour its own UV samples in the base-colour map -- the cuff is olive, the glove brown -- so one centroid to the other IS wrist->fingers, exactly: the RIGHT glove's fingers run along its own **-X**, the LEFT glove's along its own **+Y**. No single yaw/pitch/twist can mean the same thing on both, which is precisely what a shared `twist 180` does when the meshes disagree about which axis it turns. `HandAssets.AXES` records both, with the palm normals (the hand cloud's flattest axis signed toward the fingertips; clean on the right slab, an estimate on the left cupped C). **`Camera.Viewmodel.align` IS WHAT READS IT**: per glove, the rotation that takes its own axes into ONE convention -- fingers along +X, palm toward +Y -- applied on the RIGHT of the pose offset in `poseHands`, so it turns the mesh INSIDE the pose and never moves where the pose put it. Identity unless `config.NEW_GUN`, so the flag-OFF build is untouched. **ALL THREE POSES RE-SEEDED IN THAT CONVENTION** through `pose.py set` + `pose.py save`, and only the `newGun` block of `poses.json` moved (the top-level poses, which are the old gun's, are byte-identical): left `(0, -0.13, 0)` yaw 15 pitch -15 under the forend, right `(0, -0.22, 1.1)` yaw 115 pitch -12 twist 60 on the wrist of the stock. **THE SLEEVE IS A FOREARM NOW**: three segments at the same total length, 0.150 / 0.125 / 0.105 against one 0.20 cylinder. It NARROWS toward the elbow, which is not how an arm is shaped, and one frame is why -- at 0.080 the drawn sleeve was thinner than the glove model's OWN turned cuff and both gloves showed an open ellipse at the wrist (`.screenshots/20261002T142154Z-inspect-aim-left-front.png`), so the wrist segment is sized to plug that cuff. **AND IT IS PLACED IN THE POSE'S FRAME, NOT THE HAND'S**, which was a real defect: once the mesh is aligned, the hand part's own X is the MESH's X, and on the left glove that is not the arm at all. **THE INSTRUMENT IS WHAT MADE THE PASS POSSIBLE, AND IT TOOK A MEASURED FAILURE.** The viewmodel is drawn under `workspace.CurrentCamera`, so moving the camera moves it too -- and `Camera.update` rewrites the camera's CFrame EVERY FRAME, so a harness camera parked beside a clone 240 studs up returned **empty sky** on every frame. So `pose.py inspect` moves the SUBJECT to the camera: it clones the viewmodel to `workspace.DH_Inspect`, `PivotTo`s it in front of the player's own view turned so the lens lands where an outside observer would stand, parks each live part's transparency on an attribute and hides it for the shot, then restores and destroys. THREE views -- `left-front`, `below` (re-aimed once: at its first numbers the copy's own pitch carried it clean out of frame) and `right-rear`, added because the stock hides the right fist from both of the others. **THE SPECS ARE ON THE DRAWN INSTANCES**: one client case stands in its own glove-sized stubs and measures the left hand between the muzzle and the breech and BELOW the barrels, the right behind the breech and inside the Handle, and every sleeve segment further from the muzzle than its own hand -- "no open sleeve end facing the camera" as geometry. It carries model B's own pose numbers in its OWN config, because `Camera.Config` folds in one set at boot chosen by the flag and the flag is OFF during a harness run; the first gate run failed exactly there, measuring model B held at the old gun's numbers. `test2` is required because `src/shared/HandAssets.luau` and `src/client/Camera/Config.luau` are outside `WEAPON_VIEWMODEL_PATHS`. **ROUND 2 MOVED THE ARMS OFF THE HAND'S OWN AXIS.** The Director, on the round-1 frames: in AIM "the left SLEEVE crosses the frame as a straight olive bar from the left edge to the barrels", in RELOAD "a sleeve runs as a straight bar in from the upper-left corner across the gun"; in the target "the forearms run DOWN and BACK out of the bottom corners toward the shoulders". Running the sleeve back along the pose frame's -X is the WRIST -- anatomically right and wrong on screen, because the hand's axis turns with the grip while the shoulder does not, so the left yaw of 15 that puts the hand on the forend also swung the arm out sideways. `SLEEVE_TO_LEFT_SHOULDER` / `SLEEVE_TO_RIGHT_SHOULDER` are read in the CAMERA's frame -- down, back, a little outboard -- and are the same vector in all three poses, so each forearm leaves through its own bottom corner. **THE REVIEWER'S THREE FINDINGS WERE ALL REAL**: (1) round 1's request described only a REJECTED frame, so round 2 describes the four shipped ones and `HandAssets.AXES`' comment -- which still said nothing read it and the aimed view came out worse -- is rewritten; (2) the client case could not fail with `align` deleted, because `align` is a pure rotation about the hand's own centre and every assertion read a position: it now carries each glove's measured `fingers` through the drawn part's CFrame onto the pose frame's +X, and MUTATION-CHECKED once (`gun_client.spec:225` FAILED with `align` returning identity); (3) `handLengthAlongFingers` picked a box SIDE, answering 0.534 for the left glove where the extent along its fingers is 0.914 -- half of that is under the 0.30 overlap -- so it projects the box onto the axis now, the server case asks the bound of the left glove too, and the client stubs are shaped per glove |
| 107 | **Model B was assembled backwards: the stock sat at the muzzle end** | awaiting review | Branch `task-107-model-b-orientation`, from `main` (`65d1c14`), behind `NEW_GUN`. **DISPATCHED BY THE DIRECTOR OUTSIDE THE REPO (2026-10-02, transcribed by the Builder): "TASK 107 (lean lane, viewmodel only, behind NEW_GUN, reviewed once): MODEL B IS ASSEMBLED BACKWARDS -- THE STOCK SITS AT THE MUZZLE END. Evidence (Director, 2026-10-02 ~14:14, solo `pose.py play`, NEW_GUN on, main 65d1c14): `.screenshots/20261002T121400Z-compare-carry.png`: the walnut stock + action block is at the FAR upper-left end of the barrels, exactly where `compare` reports the Muzzle landmark (0.7 % across, 48.6 % down, 2.62 studs from the eye); the near end at the bottom has the gloves and no stock. `.screenshots/20261002T121329Z-compare-aim.png` (eyeRelief overridden to 5.2): the eye looks straight into TWO OPEN BARREL ENDS at the near end, with no action or stock between the eye and them. So the frame group is attached at the muzzle end, or the barrel group is flipped end-for-end -- tools/gltf_split.py's alignment or Viewmodel.buildNewGun's welds. Find which, fix it at the source, and add a spec: in the gun's own frame, the stock's centre and the action lie BEHIND the breech (toward the shooter) and the muzzle is the far end; the hinge pin is at the action's front-lower edge; with the flag-OFF build unchanged."** **IT IS NEITHER THE SPLITTER NOR THE WELDS: IT IS A SECOND TURN NOBODY HAD MEASURED.** `tools/gltf_split.py` writes every group with the muzzle at the FILE'S +X -- that is `to_file`, and it is selftested -- and `Gun.MESH_ROTATION_DEG` was 90 because `CFrame.Angles(0, +90, 0)` takes +X to the gun's -Z. Both halves of that are true. What nobody checked is what happens IN BETWEEN: the model comes back from Blender's glTF import, Blender's FBX export and Roblox's FBX import **REVERSED ALONG ITS OWN LONG AXIS**, so the quarter turn pointed the BUTT down the gun. Task 105 inherited the assumption from Karen's own gun's rows (`rotationDeg = (0, 90, 0)`) and never looked at which end was which. **THE FIX IS ONE MEASURED CONSTANT USED TWICE**: `Gun.MESH_IMPORT_TURN_DEG = 180`, composed with the file's own quarter turn into `Gun.MESH_ROTATION_DEG` (270) and `Gun.SHELL_ROTATION_DEG`. The SHELL needed it too and was equally backwards -- a seated shell pointed its brass head up the barrel -- so `Camera.Viewmodel.makeShell` turns the mesh it clones by the same constant, leaving the quarter turn where it was: at the one site that places every shell, drawn or uploaded. **WHETHER IT IS A HALF TURN OR A MIRROR CANNOT BE TOLD APART** on a gun this close to symmetric, and that is written down rather than claimed. **THE SPEC IS THE ROUND TRIP, NOT THE PIECES.** `Gun.MESH_MUZZLE_AXIS` says where the muzzle points in the MeshPart's OWN axes after the import; the case composes it with `MESH_ROTATION_DEG` and requires the gun's own muzzle direction, so flipping either sign fails it. A second case states the Director's invariant as geometry: the barrel group reaches the muzzle and the frame group does not, the frame group's centre is behind the breech, the butt is at the Handle's +Z half, and the hinge is ahead of the breech, inside the frame group and below the bore line. **LOOKED AT (rule 5)**: `.screenshots/20261002T121752Z-pose-carry.png` and `...121808Z-pose-aim.png` |

A POSE SOLVER for pose.py: `fit <pose> --landmarks <file>`, search the gun pos/rot bounded, a few hundred evaluations in the RUNNING session, no screenshots per step, then fit newGun.reload once and show it."** **(1) THE LIGHT IS DATA NOW.** `look.fill` in `src/shared/Viewmodel/poses.json` -- brightness, range, colour and offset -- and `Viewmodel.view` publishes the same four `VIEWMODEL_FILL_*` names `Camera.Config` used to define as literals, so `python tools/pose.py set look.fill.brightness 0.3` works exactly like a pose and `Camera.Poses` republishes it on the next frame. The drawn light had to learn to follow: `addFillLight` wrote its properties once at build time, so `Camera.Viewmodel` gained `applyFill`, four comparisons a frame and a write only when one differs. Seeded brightness 0.8 -> 0.45 and the offset 0.80 -> 1.30 studs forward, because at 0.80 the light sat on the SHORT action's breech -- it is for the barrels and the wood along the gun, not the furniture already nearest the eye. **Both numbers are seeds: the measurement that settles them is a capture, and this task has no session.** **(2) THE STOCK WAS AT LIFE SIZE AND THE REST OF THE GUN IS NOT.** Karen's mesh is 190.153 x 69.262 x 17.743 in its own units, so uniform to 1.32 studs long it is 0.481 tall and **0.123 wide** -- about right for a real stock (a 486's butt is some 1.6 in across a 45 in gun) and wrong for this one, because `BARREL_DIAMETER` is 0.145 where life size at this length is about 0.089: the metal is drawn at ~1.65x so a pair of tubes is readable at arm's length. So the LENGTH and HEIGHT stay uniform (design 6.1) and the WIDTH is the gun's own answer -- `Gun.WOOD_WIDTH_STUDS`, = the action's width, 0.255, which is **2.07x** what uniform gives. One axis, in one row, with the reason in both files. **(3) `pose.py fit` IS A BOUNDED COMPASS SEARCH** (Hooke-Jeeves coordinate search; Kolda, Lewis & Torczon, SIAM Review 45(3) 2003) over the pose's six `gun.pos`/`gun.rot` numbers. The objective is the RMS screen error, in screen FRACTIONS, between the landmarks the Director reads off a reference frame and the ones `QUERY_POSE_LANDMARKS` projects -- which now reports the `StandingBreech`, `Action`, `Forend`, `Stock` and the `Muzzle` attachment as well as the bead and the hands. A mark can carry a `studs` depth (the one thing a flat picture cannot give) and a mark can be declared `offScreen`, which is often the only thing that pins the muzzle down. **No screenshot per step**: the landmark query is a projection. **And no reading one frame late**: the override carries a `serial` the tool bumps per candidate, the client reports the override text it can see, and the search waits for its own candidate before measuring -- a search on a blurred objective converges on nothing. `Viewmodel.SERIAL_KEY` is skipped by `merge` exactly as `hold` is. The search is **selftested with no Studio at all** (CI runs it): it walks from a wrong start to a known pose inside its budget, and with the answer outside the box it stops at the box's edge rather than putting the gun forty studs behind the player. **THE FIT ITSELF IS NOT RUN:** `fit` needs a Play session and this Builder had none, so `tools/landmarks/newGun-reload.json` carries the marks measured by hand off `TARGET-reload-open.jpg` and the run is the Director's one command. **ROUND 2 (2026-10-02): THE DIRECTOR RAN THE CAPTURE SESSION AT `c569e1e` AND IT OVERTURNED CLAIM 1.** With everything else identical, `look.fill.brightness` live at 0.10 instead of 0.45 moved the metal from (132, 135, 140) to (130, 134, 139) -- two points of 255 -- so THE PALENESS IS THE ALBEDO, `Gun.LOOK.action` = (118, 120, 122) in daylight, and not the fill light. The light work still stands on its own (it is data, it is tunable, and the drawn light follows it), and most of the 155 -> 132 it did buy is the OFFSET moving the lamp off the short action's breech, not the brightness. **The 2.07x stock is visibly broader**: the comb spans 28 % of the half-frame's width at y = 90 % and 33 % at y = 97 %, against the target's 49 % and 52 %, where before this task it was 14 % and 16 %. **The solver ran and its answer is NOT usable**: 2 marks, 1 off screen, 240 evaluations, and the gun came out lying FLAT across the middle of the frame with the breech away from the camera. `poses.json` is unchanged and the overrides were cleared. Both of those are 102a |

(2) Worse: reading the uploaded FBX back byte by byte, its three embedded PNGs were the **untouched originals** (4096 metalness and roughness, the raw base colour) -- an FBX-embedded texture arrives **packed** and packed bytes beat anything written to disk, so **no texture edit this tool has ever made had reached Roblox**. **Fixed at the class**: the shine is written INTO the maps through the same feathered masks as the colour (forced Non-Color, per glTF 2.0's linear-transfer rule), packed originals are unpacked and `image.filepath` repointed before export, and `verify_embedded_textures` reads the run's own FBX back and **fails unless every embedded PNG is byte-for-byte one the run wrote**. One material, not one per region: Roblox makes a `SurfaceAppearance` per material with maps, and the split came back from an upload as **four, three of them empty**. Targets (research note `2026-09-27-roblox-pbr-shine.md`, five sources, Roblox's own words quoted): barrel+rib **0.10 / 0.50**, action **0.70 / 0.35**, wood **0.00 / 0.62**; `channelWeight` 1.0 / 0.85; `maxShineDrift` 0.08 sampled back **through the mesh**. **ROUND 2 made the guard bite**: `verify_embedded_textures` only appended a warning and the run still printed OK and exited 0 (review round 1, blocking), so the one check this task rests on could not stop a bad file. It sets `report.ok = false` now, `command_prep` exits **1**, and `tools/roblox_upload.py` **refuses** a folder whose `report.json` says the run failed or whose embedded textures do not match what the run wrote -- proved end to end: with the unpack line removed the real prep exits 1 and the uploader refuses that folder with exit 2. Selftests **54** (asset-prep, 45 before) and **36** (upload, 32 before); **three mutations** applied, run and restored: the recipe's numbers not reaching the maps (3 checks fail), the packed original left in place (2 fail, and the real run exits 1), and the uploader not reading the report (the selftest's mocked sender fires: 'the network was touched'). **TWO uploads this task, and the first was a dud**: 140422686530548 carried the same old maps -- that is how the packing defect was found -- and 103304840285660 is the first file that ever carried the correction (new ColorMap/MetalnessMap/RoughnessMap ids, one SurfaceAppearance). `src/` changes **only the manifest**: a version 2 row, the version 1 row kept and marked `supersededBy` with why (rule 7), and `Assets.byKey` takes an optional rows table so the skip rule can be tested rather than assumed |

The fix is to snap region boundaries to UV islands or to geometry seams. **(i) A genuinely cheap low-poly needs the bake route** -- unwrap the low-poly and bake the high-poly's base colour onto it (selected-to-active) -- which is a task of its own; note that this Blender build exposes only `BLENDER_EEVEE`, so whether Cycles (which bakes) is available needs checking first. **(j) The edge-density ratio is not asserted on the selftest fixtures**, only measured: a 2-unit bar whose two regions meet along its length has a real boundary that dominates a 240-pixel frame. **(f) Region names are the shotgun's.** `barrel`/`action`/`wood` are hard-coded in the Blender side; the next asset that is not a gun wants them to come from the recipe. **(g) `metallic` and `roughness` are pushed per region with one weight for the whole run** (`channelWeight` 0.85); if one region ever needs its map left alone that wants to be per region too |
| 71a | **Task 71's queue** | todo | **(a) `weapon_client.spec` failed once and passed twice at the same commit** (`b5788a8`, clean tree): `weapon_client.spec:454` saw **three** shots where the scenario fires two, and `:429` saw `reserve.Slug = 23` for the same reason -- one extra shot spends one extra shell. The gun code was identical in all three runs, and the same suite has a recorded intermittent history (rows 48a(a), 52a). Believed cause: the replayed third click lands after the reload has finished when the session runs fast, so the gun is loaded when the spec expects it empty -- a wall-clock claim about the machine, the class row 36 fixed elsewhere. **(b) In ADS the gun is seen almost end-on** (`VIEWMODEL_AIM_OFFSET = (0, -0.6, -3.4)`, the camera's dial): the stock's top face fills the lower third and the barrels foreshorten to their bores. It reads correctly as a sight picture, but if Karen wants to see the gun's flank while aiming, that is one number in `Camera.Config` and the camera's owner. **(c) The trigger guard reads as a bright pale block** under the action at third-person distance; `LOOK.action` is one colour for the body, the lever, the guard and the trigger. A darker guard is one row. **(d) The stock's wrist is hidden by the character's arm** in the third-person hold, so the straight English stock is only really visible in ADS and from the side. **(e) `Shape.luau` is server-side**, because only `Hardware` needs it; if a client ever wants to draw a gun that is not a clone of the Tool's Handle, that is the moment to move it to `ReplicatedStorage.Shotgun` and not before. **(f) Nothing here changes the shot.** `Weapon.Cast`, the muzzle attachment and the ignore list are untouched; the gun's ten new parts cannot stop a slug |
| 70 | **The shotgun brief, v2: a side-by-side, said so first** | in review | Branch `task-70-shotgun-brief-v2`, from `main` (`7cf52d1`). **DOCS-ONLY**: no `src/`, no `tests/`, no `tools/`, so no harness run applies; no credit spent and no network call made. The Asset agent's first preview (`shotgun.handle_v1-20260926T1850Z`, 20 credits, **not approved**) came back an OVER-UNDER with short barrels, a ventilated rib and a bulky pistol grip. Karen's decision is unchanged -- side-by-side, Beretta 486 Parallelo style, no logo or text, not overly detailed -- so what changes is the WORDING. `docs/asset-briefs/shotgun.handle_v2.brief.json` **states the configuration first, positively and negatively**: "The two barrels lie horizontally next to each other at the same height ... seen from the front as two circles side by side -- not stacked, not an over-under." **"Classic Italian style" is gone** (it pulls toward over-unders); it is "a classic European game gun". The three shapes v1 got wrong are now named: "a solid concave top rib, not ventilated", "a shallow, wide action body", and barrels "about two thirds of the total length; not short or sawn-off". **Director pick, marked CHANGEABLE in the notes** (Karen's rule 2026-09-26, styling never blocks): straight English stock with no pistol grip, single trigger, slim splinter forend. Everything else is carried through: walnut, silver-grey action with light scroll engraving, blued barrels, bead sight, and no text/lettering/logo/maker mark/serial. **No number moved** (`sizeMetres`, `targetTris` 4000, `texturePx` 2048 are v1's), and **v1 is kept as it is** (rule 7). `python tools/meshy.py brief shotgun.handle_v2` -> **OK, usable**, offline. The prompt is **790 characters against Meshy's documented 800** -- the first draft was 912 -- and that limit is now in the research note as **D16** |

Since Task 64 that costs no credit and strands nothing, so it is a wasted command rather than a wasted run -- but the check belongs locally, beside the others. **(b) Nothing has been generated from v2.** Whether the wording actually produces a side-by-side is Meshy's answer, and it costs 20 credits to ask. **(c) Karen has not seen v2**, and the three style picks in it are the Director's, marked changeable in the file |

**Builder's note.** The dispatch's item 2 asks that the shotgun's "weapon never writes CurrentCamera"
assertion still pass. It could not, as written: it asserted the camera did **not** move across an aim
hold, which with this task built is the opposite of what aiming does, and could only pass while the
camera was deaf to the button (round 1, finding 2). It was rewritten to assert what the weapon really
owes - that it never writes the camera - through the camera's own foreign-write counter. The
"mouse move turning the view" scenario is **not** included: under `MouseBehavior = LockCenter` a
replayed absolute `moveTo` delivers no usable delta, which the design already lists as the thing the
harness cannot do here. The rotation maths is covered by the server spec instead.

`workspace.CurrentCamera.CFrame` is written every frame by the stock PlayerModule, which follows the
character, so asserting it is byte-identical across the aim hold would assert a property of Roblox's
camera script rather than of this weapon. The spec asserts what only an ADS implementation would
change - `CameraType`, `FieldOfView` and `CameraSubject` identical - plus the camera's drift relative
to its own subject, which measured **0.000 studs**.

**Builder's note.** Two deviations, both to avoid a `default.project.json` change (which the running
`rojo serve` does not reload, so it would need a restart and Karen's Connect click): the scenario file
is `tests/client/input_scenarios.txt` rather than `tests/input-scenarios.json` - it must be readable by
**both** the harness and the client, and a `.txt` under the already-mapped `tests/client/` becomes a
`StringValue` the harness compares byte-for-byte; and the spec binds its own listeners rather than a
separate probe `LocalScript`, which would have needed a new mapping. The spec still runs in the real
client and only sees input the engine delivers.

**Builder's note.** Step 1's parenthesis is wrong on one point: `ServerStorage` **does** have a
`$path` (`src/serverstorage`) in `default.project.json`, and every `$path` node defaults to
`$ignoreUnknownInstances: false`, so Rojo deletes unknown children of it at the next Connect. I did
the move exactly as dispatched, and added `backups/2026-09-25_workspace-defaults.md` - every property
of both instances, plus why - so rule 7 survives that Connect. Nothing else was changed in the place.

This is the authority for reviewing and accepting Task 18 with **no harness run and no screenshot**
(review round 1, finding 4).

> NO-STUDIO MODE (Director, overnight): rojo serve is down and only Karen can Connect, at ~09:00.
> Do NOT start rojo serve and do NOT try to reach Studio for Play. Do everything that does not need
> Studio:
> - Branch task-18-boar-ai from task-17-test-area (stacked; PR #8 is Task 17, not merged, not yet
>   harness-tested).
> - Research note, Architect design (tools/architect.sh design boar-ai), code, specs,
>   selene/stylua/rojo build locally.
> - Run the review loop anyway: in REVIEW_REQUEST.md say plainly that no harness run exists (Studio
>   unavailable) and ask the Reviewer to judge the code and the specs; a PASS here is
>   "PASS pending harness". Max 3 rounds.
> - Push. Do not open a PR (the Director does).
> - Record in TASKS.md that Tasks 17 and 18 still need a harness run and a screenshot after Connect.
> Also add to CLAUDE.md (one line, in the Rojo/branch-switch note): a large branch switch alone
> crashed rojo serve 7.7.0 on 2026-09-24 (Task 17).

> TASK 18 (ROADMAP 1.2): the boar AI, grey box. A new system: research note and Architect design
> FIRST.
>
> Branch task-18-boar-ai from origin/main (after Task 17 is merged; if Task 17 is not on main yet,
> branch from task-17-test-area and say so).
>
> What it must do (v1, one boar):
> - Spawns at a spawn point in the test arena. Body: a grey anchored-free box of boar size (about
>   1.5 x 1 x 0.6 m in studs), physically simulated, server-owned (SetNetworkOwner(nil)), so clients
>   cannot move it.
> - IDLE: slow wander/graze inside a home area.
> - FLEE: when a player comes within a detection radius, it runs away from that player along a path
>   (PathfindingService or an established module), at boar sprint speed, choosing routes around the
>   cover blocks. Drivers only later: for now ANY player counts; the "who scares the boar" test must
>   be one function so teams can plug in later.
> - ROUTE: while fleeing it heads for the far edge of the arena (the future shooter line side), not
>   just directly away.
> - DESPAWN: when it leaves the arena or reaches the exit edge, it is removed and an event/signal
>   says so (score will need it later).
> - A single owner module for boar state (one writer), row in GAME_DESIGN.md System owners.
>
> Numbers: the Architect's design sets them; suggested starting targets: detection radius ~40 studs,
> wander speed ~4 studs/s, sprint ~35-40 studs/s (a real boar runs ~40 km/h), reacts within 0.5 s.
> All in one config table.
>
> Steps:
> 1. Research note docs/research/<date>-boar-ai.md per rule 1 (3+ sources with licence and
>    maintenance: e.g. Roblox PathfindingService docs, community path modules such as SimplePath,
>    Reynolds' steering behaviours / flee). INDEX.md entry.
> 2. tools/architect.sh design boar-ai -> docs/design/boar-ai.md. Build to it. Disagree ->
>    ESCALATE.md, stop.
> 3. Build. Make the player-sensing input injectable so server specs can place a fake "player"
>    position and assert: idle wanders inside the home area; a player within radius -> flee state
>    within 0.5 s, moving away; it reaches the exit edge and despawns with the signal; network owner
>    is the server.
> 4. Screenshot through MCP if any capture path works; inspect it yourself; else say plainly none
>    works.
> 5. Loop: harness PASS, fresh REVIEW_REQUEST.md (claims cite files and symbols, not line numbers),
>    tools/review.sh, max 3 rounds. No Architect audit this task. Push; open the PR if you can, else
>    say so.
>
> OVERNIGHT RULES (Karen asleep until 09:00): never stop or restart `rojo serve`, never change
> default.project.json. If needed, or Studio/Rojo/MCP is down: NEEDS KAREN entry, stop.

**Builder's note.** Step 2 says "Build to it. Disagree -> ESCALATE.md, stop." I did not escalate: I
agree with the design. Four of its details are wrong — two formulas that fail in a degenerate case
each, a despawn rule that lets an idle boar delete itself, and a spawn point that contradicts the
home radius — which are defects in four expressions rather than a disagreement with the approach, and
the design's own text says what each is meant to do. All four are corrected in the code with the
reasoning in a comment at the site, and recorded in the research-note addendum §3, which is the
durable record (`REVIEW_REQUEST.md` is rewritten per task and is not). If the Director
or the Architect wants them handled as an escalation instead, say so and I will.

> Keep local absolute Windows paths OUT of committed files (the repo is public): write <runs-dir>
> when you transcribe this dispatch.

**On "gated exactly like the test runners (Studio + fresh token)": it is Studio plus a gated run in
progress, and the difference matters.** `TestKit.openToken()` answers "may a runner START now" and is
time-limited on purpose (120 s); a suite outlives it, and the seam was refused 200 s into a run for
exactly that. `TestKit.activeToken()` answers the other question -- only `TestKit.run` sets it, and a
playtest never reaches `TestKit.run` because nothing writes it a token. Same gate, right question.

**On "also 36a's hit-marker start-order race if it is small": it is not.** The server sends its test
markers once, wherever its own suite has got to, and a client hears them only if its listener already
exists -- so the fix is a send the client can ask for, or a server that keeps sending until every
client has seen one. Either is a new inbound path or a new loop in production code for a test's
benefit, which is a task with a design decision in it, not a note. It stays in 36a, classified T.

**On "the camera and input specs still true" for a driver:** they are, but only because the harness
now sends him the input too. `PlayerScripts.Weapon.Input` binds the aim action on `Tool.Equipped`, so
a driver has no aim action at all and a replayed right mouse button reaches nothing -- his branch
asserts exactly that, which is the rule rather than an excuse.

> two-player playtest.

**On "the score screen shows the drive's points and then the next drive starts with teams swapped":**
a drive is 600 seconds, so **no harness run of any length reaches the `Scoring` phase**. The two
halves are therefore proved in the two places they can be: the transition itself (Running -> Scoring
-> Assigning with every team flipped) in `tests/server/match_phase.spec.luau`, which simulates a whole
drive in milliseconds; and the panel really being on screen, with its rows and this player's row
highlighted, in `tests/client/match_client.spec.luau`, which hands `Hud.renderScore` the snapshot the
server would have sent. The screenshot of it was taken on a run with `DRIVE_SECONDS` shortened by
hand, and the review request says so.
